data-reset=\"65\">清空本章作答，重新自测</button><div class=\"question\" id=\"q-66-1\" data-question=\"65,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. scope</p><p><strong>How does Mara respond to the claim that Elena&#x27;s clip is already circulating?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. She announces that every source used by the studio is fabricated.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. She accepts that circulation gives the studio unrestricted permission.</button></div><div class=\"feedback\" hidden=\"hidden\"></div></div></section><nav class=\"chapter-nav\" aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"64\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"66\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"v06c07","title":"Still Her Father's Move","volume":"卷六｜被盗名字的审判","anchor":"chapter-0067","paragraphs":["Leda brought the broken chess knight in a cake tin. She had considered wrapping it in silk, then decided that her father had never trusted anything too carefully displayed.","\"He'll ask where the cake is,\" she said.","Amina Holt met them outside a small accommodation center beyond the metropolitan boundary. The building had once housed a civil-service training school. It now offered short stays arranged through independent advocates. A faded announcement beside the entrance invited residents to an aerobic class. Somebody had added, in pencil, WALKING THERE COUNTS.","\"I like the place already,\" Mara said.","Leda tried to smile. Her hands were shaking around the tin.","The man had asked for Leda, Noor, Amina, and Mara. Cassian would wait outside unless invited. There would be no journalistic recording. Amina had checked identity through the private records available to her and spoken to him more than once. He knew why they were coming and could end the meeting. Mara put away her phone before they went upstairs.","Tomas Reed sat beside a window overlooking a narrow yard. He was thinner than the man Mara remembered from Kingfisher, with a dark belt holding trousers that no longer fitted. A cup of coffee stood untouched on the table. When the door opened, he rose too quickly and had to catch the chair.","Leda stopped a step inside.","For a moment neither spoke. Then Reed looked at the tin.","\"You haven't made that awful lemon cake again?\"","The sound that came out of Leda was halfway between laughter and a sob. \"You ate three pieces.\"","\"A father has responsibilities.\"","She crossed the room. He folded her into his arms with such awkward eagerness that the tin tipped and the chess piece rolled across the floor. Mara caught it before it reached the radiator. Nobody noticed. Leda was holding her father's jacket as if the fabric were the only thing keeping the room together.","Mara set the knight on the table and turned towards the window. She had imagined a reunion like this during the years she thought Elena was gone. The real thing had no music and no perfect sentence. It had a daughter trying to breathe and a man whispering that he was sorry.","Amina let them have the silence.","After a while, Leda pulled a chair close. \"You were allowed to tell me you wanted to leave.\"","\"They said you were frightened of me getting confused.\"","\"I was frightened because nobody would let me ask you.\"","Reed closed his eyes. \"I kept thinking I must have agreed to things. There were forms with my name. There were people who sounded so certain. Then I'd remember a morning differently and think I was becoming wicked for doubting them.\"","Noor sat where he could see her. She did not begin with a test. She asked whether he was comfortable, whether he had eaten, and what he wanted from the visit. He wanted to remain with his daughter for a while. After that, he wanted the papers he had hidden to be found.","\"I can't remember every session,\" he said. \"I remember knowing that I wouldn't remember. That was why I wrote things down.\"","Mara felt the room tighten around the words. She kept her own questions behind them. He was a human being trying to describe a missing part of his life, not an instrument that would produce proof if she pressed the right place.","Leda put the knight in his hand. The bird on its broken head was crooked. He turned it over and touched the old glue.","\"You said the king needed someone who could see over the wall,\" she told him.","\"You said birds didn't need permission.\"","Her smile trembled. \"I was a very difficult child.\"","\"An achievement you have maintained.\"","The little exchange restored something the formal records could not. He was her father. She could be angry, relieved, and loving at once. None of those feelings needed to overrule the others.","Leda opened a second package she had brought: bread, cheese, and a little jar of the jam her father used to like. He looked at the jar as though it had crossed a great distance to reach him.","\"We can have ordinary days,\" she said. \"They won't all need an advocate and an announcement.\"","\"You make it sound like a government program.\"","\"There will be rules. You will answer your phone. I will stop pretending I don't need to hear you complain about the weather.\"","He nodded, smiling through the tiredness. \"A severe regime.\"","\"A family,\" she said. \"You may have forgotten how troublesome they are.\"","Mara turned the cup so its handle faced his stronger hand. His fingers settled around it, and the room began to feel less like the end of a search than the beginning of somebody's afternoon.","When he was ready, Reed described the journey from the depot. A woman cleaning the platforms had seen him struggling with a ticket machine. She wore a green coat over her work clothes. He asked for directions to the theater, and she walked with him because her shift was over. She had not known what he was escaping.","\"She thought I was going to a performance,\" he said. \"I let her think it.\"","Amina had located the cleaner through a permitted inquiry. Her account of the route and the time fitted the depot records. There had been no secret official escort after all, just somebody willing to help a stranger get onto the right bus.","Reed had not gone directly to Leda because he was convinced a visit would draw attention to her. He had spent nights in places where old railway colleagues let him sleep, moving when he thought he had been recognized. Fear had made his world smaller and his plans more elaborate. At last a former colleague persuaded him to speak to Amina rather than continue living inside his own precautions.","\"I thought keeping away was the virtuous choice,\" he said.","Leda took his hand. \"Please let me vote next time.\"","He gave a short, broken laugh. \"A democratic household. That will take adjustment.\"","Amina had requested Reed's belongings for delivery to the reception desk. She had not requested transport for him.","The knock came before Mara had asked about the folder.","A man stood in the hall with a carrier bag and an expression of bureaucratic patience. He said he had come from the foundation's welfare team. Mr. Reed had medication and belongings at his former residence. The team could arrange transport back that afternoon.","Amina remained in the doorway. \"He has not requested transport.\"","\"He may not understand the arrangements.\"","Reed heard. His hand tightened around Leda's. For a second Mara saw the frightened man from Kingfisher again, preparing to let somebody else's certainty fill the space where his own should be.","Then he stood.","\"I understand that I don't want to go with you,\" he said.","The man looked past Amina. \"Your care plan—\"","\"Can be discussed with me. Here. When I am ready. Not in a car I haven't chosen.\"","Noor moved beside him, not in front. The foundation worker hesitated. Amina asked him to leave the bag and provide a contact for the medication records. She would arrange a proper transfer of information with Reed's agreement. If he had an order requiring something else, he could produce it through the appropriate channel.","He had no order. He left the bag.","Reed sat down slowly. Leda touched his shoulder. \"You sounded like yourself.\"","\"I've had some practice,\" he said, and wiped his face with a handkerchief.","Inside the bag were slippers, a paperback, and a photograph he thought had been lost. He held the photograph for a long time. It showed Leda as a pre-teen beside a miniature stage, wearing a crown too large for her head. On the back, someone had written a childhood address.","Mara gave him time. Outside, rain began to mark the window. There were documents to retrieve, a court to persuade, and a city allowing private fear to become public policy. For ten minutes, none of them needed to lead the charge.","At last Reed turned to her. \"The folder you asked about. At the old warehouse. I put it in a scenery case.\"","\"Which one?\"","\"The rain machine. The old theater one. Metal drum, wood frame. They used to turn it behind the stage to make a storm. I left the cards in the hollow end, behind a repair panel.\"","Mara remembered the rows of cases at the Old Exchange. \"Why there?\"","\"Because they would take a medical file. They wouldn't notice a broken storm. I wrote the dates of the sessions. What I remembered before. What was missing afterward. Names of people who were actually in the room. Not what I guessed later.\"","Leda looked at Mara, and the old fear passed between them: would they arrive in time?","Amina called the warehouse caretaker. Peter Moss answered on the third attempt. The rain machine had gone back to the theater's scenery store after the last delivery. It was scheduled for use that night in the revival of the old exile play.","Mara stared at the chess knight, the cake tin, and the father sitting beside his daughter.","\"Then,\" she said, \"I suppose we're going to the theater.\""],"questions":[{"type":"inference","stem":"What does Reed's response to the foundation worker show?","options":["He no longer needs support from anyone.","He wants his own care and movements discussed with him rather than decided over him.","He is ready to give a complete technical account of every session.","He has agreed to let the worker decide where he should The stage manager considered the storm essential. He considered the discovery of potentially important papers inside it a badly timed hobby.","\"Act Two,\" he said. \"You can open it after Act Two. Before then, the weather belongs to me.\"","Leda had remained with her father. Mara showed the manager the permission she had sent, along with Reed's request. Cassian held the tools. They were granted a corner behind the curtain and strict instructions to keep out of the moving scenery.","The place smelled of damp cloth, dust, and the softener used on a mountain of costumes. A painted dome waited beside a foam arch. The arch's polyurethane surface had begun to crumble, leaving little yellow fragments on the floor. Mara stepped around them and found the old rain drum in its wooden frame.","A replacement panel covered one end. The screws were familiar: mismatched heads, a narrow seam, and a small repair made by somebody who expected to undo it later. She could open it quickly once the manager released the machine. Until then, it had to make rain.","Cassian looked towards the auditorium. \"We might actually watch part of a play.\"","\"A pleasurable evening. An adventure for us.\"","He settled beside her on a box marked FRAGILE MOON. She moved close enough to steal his warmth. Through a gap in the scenery, they could see a narrow segment of the stage. The queen entered in the yellow coat Mara had borrowed during her escape. She looked markedly more majestic without a basket of carrots.","\"Its record has improved,\" Cassian whispered.","Mara bit his shoulder through the coat to stop herself laughing aloud. He gave her a look that promised retaliation when they were somewhere less public.","A young stagehand came to collect the rain machine. He was nervous about touching the papers and knocked his elbow against the frame. Mara steadied it before the wheels could turn.","\"Just the handle,\" she said. \"I'll keep the frame still.\"","The sound began softly. Dried material rolled inside the drum, making a whisper that grew into a downpour as he turned it. Onstage, the exile raised his face to the imaginary sky. Mara could hear the old tune beneath the orchestra, the one Reed had sung when he seemed unable to say what he needed.","Then two men came through the service door.","One carried a delivery form. The other was the man who had stood at the end of the lane outside Mara's workshop.","Cassian saw him at the same moment. His hand closed over Mara's, briefly, and released. They had no need to invent a silent system. There was a suspicious man, a machine, and only one narrow route between them.","The man with the form asked the stagehand for the item marked on his list. Rain mechanism. Academy loan. Return collection. The order looked official enough to make the boy hesitate.","\"After the performance,\" the stagehand whispered.","\"Now. The vehicle is waiting.\"","Cassian stood. \"Then the vehicle can wait a little longer.\"","The second man recognized Mara. He moved towards the drum, and the stagehand let go of the handle. The rain stopped in the middle of the exile's speech.","From the stage came a furious improvisation: \"Even heaven has deserted me!\"","Mara caught the handle and gave it one full turn. Thunderous rain returned. The audience applauded the effect. Cassian had placed himself between the men and the machine, apparently absorbed in a polite inquiry about their authorization. His voice was quiet enough not to reach the auditorium. The expression behind it was not polite at all.","Mara had no intention of leaving the papers inside while the argument became a contest of shoulders. She crouched behind the frame. The panel was below the mechanism and clear of the moving parts. She set her tool in the first screw.","Three turns. A little resistance. The stagehand, seeing what she intended, took the handle back. Rain steadied above her head. She removed the second screw, supporting the panel with her knee.","The hollow end contained a flat packet wrapped in an old theater poster. She recognized the fold from Reed's description. She drew it out and slid it inside her coat before replacing the panel.","The second man made a sudden move. Cassian caught his wrist and turned him away from the frame, not roughly enough to throw him into the scenery and not gently enough to invite another attempt. The delivery form fell to the floor.","Mara stood with the empty tool case in her hand.","\"If you want the machine,\" she said, \"you can collect it after the weather has finished.\"","The man stared at her coat. The stage manager arrived behind him, silently furious at everything that had happened to his second act. June followed, having entered through the public door at Mara's message. She held up her phone.","\"The theater has called the relevant office,\" she said. \"They are checking the collection order. I suggest you wait where the security camera can see you.\"","The men chose to leave instead. Cassian did not chase them into a crowded theater with an unprotected packet between them and the exit. He photographed the dropped form, alerted the arriving security officer, and returned to Mara.","Onstage, the exile discovered that his queen had spent the previous seven years running the kingdom more competently than he had. The audience gave her an enthusiastic reception. Mara found this a deeply encouraging development in dramatic law.","They waited for the interval before leaving. The stage manager checked the repaired panel, demanded the return of the screws, and accepted a promise that somebody would explain the delivery order in writing. He also asked whether the next important witness might hide records in something that did not need to perform on Saturday nights.","\"I'll pass on the grievance,\" Mara said.","They took the sealed packet to Amina, where Reed could identify it before anything was copied. His session cards were inside, with dates, observations, and a few receipts that helped place him at particular buildings. Names of other participants remained covered while Noor arranged the appropriate review. Reed wanted his own account available to the inquiry. Leda sat beside him while he said so.","One card made Cassian go still.","It recorded an interview after the old fire, attributed to him as the officer conducting it. A small note below described a demonstration before the questions began. The date corresponded to a period in his own account that had never fitted comfortably around the medical record.","He asked for a copy through the established process. When he handed back the original, Mara saw the careful steadiness of his fingers.","She waited until they were outside.","\"You remembered something?\"","\"I recognized something. That isn't the same.\"","He sounded tired rather than evasive. The old uncertainty had lived with him for years; now it had a shape other people could examine. He could no longer remain only the investigator asking questions about someone else's missing time.","On the way home, she asked what stepping aside would actually cost him.","\"The appointment. Access that belongs to the office rather than to me. The ability to choose how this part of the inquiry proceeds.\"","\"Will I have to call you Mister Vey?\"","He looked at her. \"Only if you want to alarm my aunt. The title is ceremonial. It won't fall off because I sign a letter.\"","\"A pity. She might become more manageable.\"","His smile appeared, then faded. Mara put her hand over his. She was not trying to make the decision painless. She wanted him to know that the man beside her would remain recognizable when the official papers changed.","At Vey House, Mara found him standing beside the workshop window. His official appointment letter lay on the table, open beside the copied card. He had written a short request to be removed from the inquiry while giving evidence as a witness. It was not yet sent.","\"Tomorrow,\" he said, \"I need to stop deciding which questions other people should answer.\"","She came to stand beside him. \"And answer some yourself.\"","\"Yes.\"","There was fear in the admission. She liked him better for allowing her to hear it. She took the pen from his hand and set it down, then drew him towards her by the open collar of his shirt.","\"Then tomorrow we do that,\" she said.","His forehead rested against hers. \"You make it sound simple.\"","\"No. I make it sound like something happening before supper. You are coming home afterward. I have become accustomed to complaining about your cooking.\"","The breath he let out was almost a laugh. He kissed her slowly, without the public composure that could make him seem unreachable. She held him until the stiffness left his shoulders.","On the table, the appointment letter remained unsigned. For the rest of that evening, it would wait."],"questions":[{"type":"scope","stem":"What does Cassian intend by stepping aside from the inquiry?","options":["To give evidence as a witness without data-score=\"67\"></p><button type=\"button\" data-reset=\"67\">清空本章作答，重新自测</button><div class=\"question\" id=\"q-68-1\" data-question=\"67,0\"><p class=\"meta\">1. scope</p><p><strong>What does Cassian intend by stepping aside from the inquiry?</strong></p><div class=\"options\"><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"0\">A. To give evidence as a witness without continuing to direct this part of the investigation.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"1\">B. To abandon Mara and leave the city permanently.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"2\">C. To prevent the court from examining the recovered card.</button><button type=\"button\" data-option=\"3\">D. Admiral immediately put his nose on it.","\"The government has accepted your resignation,\" Mara said.","\"Its representative appears to be considering the lining.\"","He moved the pass out of reach. The request had been accepted before seven. He would cease to direct the inquiry while giving evidence about his own involvement. The access attached to the appointment would end with it. A colleague had collected the official equipment without either a ceremonial speech or an attempt to pretend the choice cost nothing.","Mara fastened the silver feather pin at her collar. The original flower was still in neutral custody. She had stopped reaching for it every few minutes, though the absence remained noticeable when she was afraid.","Cassian watched her in the mirror. \"You don't have to sit through all of it.\"","\"I have brought a notebook and an unreasonable quantity of biscuits. My preparations would be wasted.\"","He came behind her and rested his chin against her hair. For a moment they looked like people considering whether to go back to bed. Then his phone vibrated with the car's arrival, and he kissed her once before letting her go.","Outside the court, a reporter shouted that the duke had been stripped of his powers. Another asked whether Mara had persuaded him to sacrifice his career. She looked towards Cassian. He gave the first reporter a mild expression.","\"An appointment isn't a crown. I have surrendered the appointment. The rest appears to be entertaining speculation.\"","Mara took his hand. They went inside together.","The public session concerned the records after the old fire and their connection to the research program. The court had appointed an independent team to examine the material from the greenhouse, the hospital transfers, and the preserved originals. Today's witnesses would explain what they knew and where their accounts failed. Selene's counsel had asked for the proceedings to be restricted because company methods were commercially sensitive. The judge permitted protection of particular technical details, not a prohibition on questions.","Mara sat between June and Rowan. Tessa Roe waited near the witness entrance with a repaired equipment case beside her feet. The engineer had survived the island. The original experiment records had mostly not. What remained would have to carry the weight of its actual survival, rather than the story everybody wanted it to tell.","Cassian's name was called.","He took the oath without his pass in his pocket. Mara saw him look once towards her. She did not smile bravely at him. She lifted the notebook so he could see the biscuit packet beneath it. His mouth moved, almost imperceptibly, and then he faced the bench.","He described the rescue after the fire, the interviews attributed to him, and the gaps he had once treated as ordinary confusion after injury. He had relied on official records to fit those gaps into a sequence. Later discrepancies had made that reliance untenable. Reed's dated cards did not supply a complete substitute. They gave independent points against which the official account could be tested.","\"Are you asserting that your memory was deliberately altered?\" counsel asked.","\"I am describing losses and inconsistencies that require investigation. I cannot ascertain every cause by remembering harder.\"","The answer annoyed the lawyer. Mara could see it in the neat alignment of his papers.","A clinical examination report was supplied under the arrangements agreed with the court. The examiner had no role in the original program. The report described present difficulties with particular intervals and the limits of retrospective assessment. It did not claim to reconstruct a whole missing afternoon. Cassian acknowledged those limits before counsel could use them as a revelation.","\"Then this could be convenient uncertainty,\" the lawyer said.","\"It could be convenient for many people if uncertainty prevented a question. I am here because it shouldn't.\"","The judge asked counsel to identify the record he wished to challenge.","A page appeared on the screen. It attributed a statement about volunteers' freedom to leave to Cassian. He recognized his signature. He did not recognize the final wording. The original had been modified through an administrative process whose history was among the materials preserved for examination. His memory would not settle its authorship. The surviving versions might.","\"You signed something,\" the lawyer said.","\"Yes. I intend to answer for what I signed. I will not invent the missing section to make myself look consistent.\"","Mara felt the words in her own empty collar. He was doing what he had watched her do: keeping responsibility separate from surrender to somebody else's creation.","The lawyer turned towards the personal relationship. Did he expect the court to accept a supposedly neutral officer's judgement when he lived with the woman who had taken the mechanism?","Cassian's expression changed. Not much. Enough.","\"I disclosed the relationship. I have left the role from which I could direct the relevant inquiry. You may question my actions. Mara is not an explanation for events that occurred years before we met.\"","Rowan shifted beside Mara, deeply satisfied. June kept writing. Mara concentrated on not looking as though she wanted to kiss the witness in full view of the court.","Before Tessa was called, a court attendant asked whether she needed to be introduced as a survivor of the island incident. Tessa looked down at the case by her feet.","\"Introduce me as an engineer,\" she said. \"The survival was useful, but it isn't my qualification.\"","Mara heard it from the corridor and smiled. Rowan offered to carry the case. Tessa gave it to him without making the acceptance into a test of pride. The burn on her sleeve had been neatly patched; the equipment inside had not been made presentable for the cameras.","\"Is it heavy?\" he asked.","\"Only when somebody expects it to explain everything.\"","He looked at her, then at the courtroom door. \"I'll try to reduce the load.\"","It was a small exchange, almost lost in the movement of people. Mara kept it with the things worth remembering about the day.","Tessa's evidence followed. She identified surviving components, the island's damaged storage material, and the difference between a nearby device and the city's communication network. A network could coordinate stations. It could not make an unpaired stranger's entire life available merely because he walked under an antenna. The proposal before the city concerned a wider use of connected equipment, which was precisely why independent scrutiny mattered.","The judge requested preservation of the original technical specifications and independent access sufficient to examine the claims. The company lawyer objected to the commercial loss. Tessa folded her hands over the case.","\"Some of the people who might answer your questions have already lost more than a specification,\" she said.","The judge made no speech. She directed that a workable access arrangement be submitted that day.","At the recess, Cassian came into the side corridor. His shoulders were straight in the old habitual way. Mara waited until the door had closed, then put both arms around him.","\"Your audience behaved better than mine,\" she said into his coat.","\"A disputed assessment.\"","\"Nobody showed a picture of your mother without permission. I give them points.\"","His arms tightened. She could feel how tired he was. For all the pressure of the questions, the difficult moment had been the one in which he admitted that he could no longer trust an account built partly from his own name. She could not give him a complete past. She could give him somewhere to stand while he looked at it.","\"Come and eat,\" she said.","\"I thought you had biscuits.\"","\"June ate two. Rowan claims that political thinking requires the third. We have reached an emergency.\"","He laughed then, properly, and she felt her own breath loosen.","The afternoon produced orders, not a final victory. Independent examination would continue. The reports and certified copies would remain protected under the court's control. A company request to move everything into its own review unit was refused. The original flower could be considered for return after the next day's documented inspection, provided a suitable custody arrangement preserved access to it.","Mara read that paragraph three times before looking up.","\"Tomorrow,\" Cassian said.","The word held so many promises that she decided not to ask it for another.","At the gate, a small group of Mercy House residents waited. Gideon held a placard saying WE HAVE QUESTIONED HIS COOKING. IT DOES NOT DISCREDIT HIS EVIDENCE. Sister Agnes had refused to let him bring a larger one.","Cassian stared at it. \"A formidable ally.\"","\"You should try the opposition,\" Gideon said.","Mara laughed so hard that the nearest reporter lost the chance to ask his prepared question. She took Cassian's hand and led him down the steps. There was still work, and there were still things to fear. But the man beside her had answered in his own voice and come back when the questions ended.","That was a good place to begin supper."],"questions":[{"type":"inference","stem":"Why does Cassian refuse to fill the missing section of his account with a consistent story?","options":["He wants to transfer responsibility for every action to Mara.","He wants responsibility assessed against actual evidence rather than an invented completion of his memory.","He has decided that no surviving record should be examined.","He believes uncertainty proves that he could not have signed anything."],"answer":1,"explanation":"他承认签过某些材料，也愿意回答自身行为；但不愿为了显得连贯而编造缺失部分，要求用存留版本和其他记录核查。","evidence":[26],"shuffled":true}],"wordCount":1499,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0069\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">卷六｜被盗名字的审判</div><h1>The Man without His Badge</h1><p id=\"chapter-0069-p1\">Cassian <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4751\" data-words=\"4751\">laid</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3941\" data-words=\"3941,3907\">his</a> investigator&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5985\" data-words=\"5985\">pass</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-928\" data-words=\"928\">beside</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1094\" data-words=\"1094\">breakfast</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6204\" data-words=\"6204\">plate</a>.
